MAGNETICALLY COUPLED ARRANGEMENT 
FOR A DRIVING AND A DRIVEN MEMBER 


FIELD OF THE INVENTION 
Backgrouna of the Invenuon 


The invention relates to s magnetically coupled ar- 
fangement operable by a pressure medium, and for use 
im a conveying system. 

ically coupled arrangements of this type are 
primarily used in conveying systems, having a driven 
assembly connected with a carmage for the goods to be 
conveyed. In this way, even goods of large size can be 
conveyed in a way similar to a pneumatic tube con- 
veyor system. 
In order to ensure trouble-free operation, the known 
magnetically coupled arrangement requires a greater 
degree of radial play between the adjacent surfaces of 
the magnet and the tube. It was generally 
agreed that this would be the only way of coping with 
the unavoidable surface roughness and cross-sectional 
irregularities of the tube. On the other hand, in the 
interests of increasing the magnetic drive force between 
the piston and the driven assembly, the total air gap 
between the two magnet arrangements would have to 
be kept as small as possible. A design incorporating a 
small air gap would require smaller, and thus lighter and 
substantially cheaper magnet arrangements than those 
used to date to achieve the required dnve force between 
the piston and the dnven assembly of the linear motor. 

It was recognised that the non-magnetic materials 
available today have surface qualities good enough to 
enabie by this alone a substantial reduction in the air 
gaps between magnet arrangements and tube surfaces in 
comparison with pnor art, though unacceptabi¢ faults 
invariably developed in practical operation. These 
faults, however, have now been found to be due not to 
surface roughness and cross-sectional irregularities (su- 
perior surface quality can now be achieved even in 
butt-welided tube sections), but rather to the presence of 
dirt and impurities on the tube surfaces, which cannot 
pass through the small gaps between magnet arrange- 
ments and tube surfaces but tend to be wedged in said 


ps 

According to the present invention there is provided 
a magnetically coupled arrangement for use in a con- 
veying system, said motor being operabie by a pressure 
medium and comprising a tubular part connectible to a 
source of the pressure medium, a piston which is slid- 
able in said tubular part and which has sealing means at 
each end for wiping engagement with an internal sur- 
face of the tubular part and so as to form a seal for the 
pressure medium, and a driven assembly which is slid- 
abie on the tubular part and which has means at each 
end for wiping engagement with an external surface of 
the tubular part, the piston and the driven assembly 
each carrying a drive magnet arrangement in the form 
of a hollow cylindncal assembly, cach magnet arrange- 
ment having radial play relative to the adjacent surface 
of the tubular part. and surfaces of the magnet arrange- 
ments which face the tubular part being closely adja- 
cent to the respective surfaces of the tubular part. 

Preferably the wiping means of the dnven assembly 
and the sealing means of the piston lie in the same plane 
transversal to the asis of the tubular part. The contact 
pressure of the wiping means and sealing means does 
not exert a torsional deforming moment on the tubular 
part, which should preferably have thin wails in order 


35 


45 


2 
to ensure a small total air gap between the two magnet 
astanger-cats. 

Preferably also, the sealing means of the piston com- 
prise sealing rings and the piston is provided with slid- 
ing guide rings near the sealing rings. The advantage of 
this is that a relatively soft material can be used for the 
psston sealing rings, which no longer have to perform 
any ‘ignificant guiding functions. In this way, friction 
koass's in the motor can be reduced to a minimum. 

Te tubular part may be made of a thin, stainless 
maternal such as non-magnetic special stee!, brass or a 
plastics material, and water or a mixture of water and 
oil may be used as the pressure medium. The movement 
of the piston and of the driven assembly is initiated 
unmediately the pressure medium is admitted and 
stopped as soon as the admission of pressure medium 
ceases, which permits extremely accurate positioning of 
the driven assembly by controlling the pressure medium 
supply. The pressure of the medium does not have to be 
high, since the piston is moved more by being carried 
along in the medium flow than by the admission of 
differential pressure. 

If the driven assembly is provided with a sleeve made 
of a magnetisabie material, which encircies the hollow 
cylindrical assembly of the magnet arrangement, mag- 
netic leakage fields in the vicinity of the driven assem- 
bly can be kept to a minimum. In this way, undesirable 
braking forces, which would otherwise be generated 
while piston anc driven assembly past magnetisable 
machine components, are avoided. By selecting a son- 
magnetisable materia! for the piston budy, a more inho- 
mogeneous magnetic field is generated in the space 
defined by the driven assembly, a favourable develop- 
ment in the interests of increasing the drive force. 

Advantageously the magnet arrangements are repre- 
sented by radially polarised annular magnets arranged 
in axial sequence and separated from each other by 
non-magnctisabie spacer mngs. The segmented siruc- 
ture of the magnet arrangements coniributes to the 
creation of an axially inhomogeneous magnetic field, 
which again increases the drive force exerted on the 
driven assembly by the piston. 

If the pis.on carnes a flexible buffer at each end, the 
peripheral surface of which is spaced radially from the 
internal surface of the tubular part. the pision can be 
stopped very accurately by running into a ngid obsta- 
cle. A further advantage of this development lies in the 
fact that a piston of large overall axial dimensions can be 
constructed, which can pass through curved tube sec- 
tions inspite of the smail radia! distance between the 
internal magnet arrangement and the internal tube sur- 
face. In thes way a long magnetically coupied arrange- 
ment compnising articulated piston sections and corre- 
sponding magnetically coupled driven assemblies tilt- 
able relat:ve to each other is achieved. With a magneti- 
cally coupled arrangeme=t of this design. a large load is 
distributed over a long tube section, the drive force 
between piston and dnven assembly is satisfactory, and 
the piston can pass th: ough curved tube sections.

| Background 0 tha Onvention 


J magnetically coupled Arrangem 
The invention relates to a Pn. ree iangg a by a 


pressure medium, and for use in a conveying - ial 


Magnetically Coupled arvamqemerts 


sbnene—Bovere | of this type are primarily used in 


conveyim, systems, having a driven assembly connected with 


“AD oe, 


a carriage for the goods to be conveyed. in this way, even 
fpoods oi large size can be conveyed in a way similar to a 


pneumatic tube conveyor system. 


. In order to ensure trouble-iree operation, the known 
magnehcatly coupled avvengement 

greoquises a greater degree of radial play between 
the sein? surfaces oi the magnet arrangements and the tube. 
It was wenerally agreed that this would be the only way of 
coping; with the unavoidable surface roughness and cross 
sectional irregularities of the tube. On the other hand, 
in the interests of increasing the magnetic drive force 
between the piston and the driven assembly, the total air 
gap between the two magnet arrangements would have to be 
kept as small as possible. A design incorporating a small 
air gap would require smaller, and thus lighter and sub- 
stuntially cheaper magnet arrangements than those used to 


date to achieve the required drive force between the piston 


and the driven assembly of the linear motor. 


I-14 


Pp 


magnetically Cougl 


It. was recop;nised that the nen-angnetic naterials 
available today have surface qualities good enough to enable 
by this alone a substantial reduction in the air gaps between 
magnet arrangements and tube surfaces in compurison with 
prior art, though unacceptable faults invariably developed 
in practical operation. These faults, however, have now been 
found to be due not to suriace roughness and cross-sectional 
irregularities (superior surface quality can now be achieved 
even in butt-welded tube sections), but rather to the presence 
of dirt and impurities on the tube suritaces, which cannot 
pass throuh Lhe small gaps between mapnet arrangements and 


tube surfaces but tend to be wedged in said gaps. 


According, to ~ present invention there is povided a 
scneee-aeter, See ae ay conveyim; system, said motor being 
operable by a pressure medium and comprising a tubular part 
connectible to a source of the pressure medium, a piston 
which is slidable in said tubular part and which has sealing 
means at each end for wiping engagement with an internal 
surface of the tubular part and so as to form a seal for 

the pressure medium, and a driven essembly which is slidable 
on the tubular part and which has means at each end for 
wiping, engagement with an external surface of the tubular 
part, the piston and the driven assembly each carrying a 
drive magnet arrangement in the form of a hollow cylindrical 
assembly, each magnet arrangement having radial play relative 
to the adjacent surface of the tubular part, and surfaces 

of the magnet. arrangements which face the tubular part 

being closely adjacent to the respective surfaces of the 


tubular part. 0-15 


-4- 


Preferably Lhe wiping means oj the driven assembly 
and the sealing, means of the piston lie in the same plane 
trensversal to the axis of the tubular part. The contact 
pressure oj the wiping means and sealing means does not 
exert a torsional deiorming, moment ou the tubular part, 
which should preferably have thin walls if order to ensure 


a small total air gap between the two magnet arrangements. 


FPreterably also, the sealing means of the piston 
comprise sealing rings and the piston is provided with 
sliding; guide rings near the sealing, rings. The advantage 
of this is that a relatively soit material can be used for 
the piston sealing, rings, which no longer have to perlorm 
any significant guiding functions. ln this way, friction 


losses in the motor can be reduced to a minimum. 


The tubular part may be made of a thin, stainless 


material such as non-magnetic special steel, brass or a 


plastics material, and water or a mixture of water and oil | 


may be used as the pressur. medium. ‘the movemeut of the 
piston and of the driven assembly is initiated immediately 


the pressure medium is admitted and stopped as soon as the 


admission of pressure medium ceases, which permits extemely 


accurate positioning, of the driven assembly by controlling 
the pressure medium supply. ‘the pressure of the medium 
does not have to be high, since the piston is moved more 
by being; carried along in the medium jlow than by the 


admission of difierential pressure.

"r™* ee 


if the driven assembly is provided with a sleeve 
mode of a maj:nelLisable material, which encircles the 
hollow cylindrical assembly of the magnet arrangement, 
magnetic leakage fields in the vicinity of the driven 
assembly can be kept to a minimum. In this way, undesirable 
braking forces, which would otherwise be generated while 
piston and driven assembly pass magnetisable machine components, 
are avoided. Ky selecting a non-maguetisable material for the 
piston body, a more inhomogeneous magnetic field is generated 
in the space defined by the driven assembly, a tavourable 


development in the interests of increasing the drive force. 


Advantageously the magnet arramjements are represented 
by radially polarised annular magnets arranged in axial 
sequence and sepurated from each other by non-magnetisable 
spacer rings. the segmented structure of the magnet arrange- 
ments conlributes to the creation of an axially inhomogeneous 
magnetic field, which again increases the drive force exerted 


on the driven assembly by the piston. 


It the piston carries a flexible buifer at each end, 
the peripheral surface of which is spaced radially from the 
internal surface of the tubular part, the piston orahe 
stopped very atéCurately by running into a rigid obstacle. 

A further advantage of this development lies in the fact 
that a piston ol large overall axial dimensions can be 
constructed, which can pass through curved tube sections 
inspite oi the small radial distance between the internal 


magnet arrangement and the internal tube surface. In this

The drawing; illustrales y* pemeumieeaend operated by a 
pressure medium, a relatively thin-walled and narrow tube 10 
beim; made of a stainless, non-mugnetic material such as non-)

preferred with glass reinforcement. The tube is made up of 
butt-welded or joined tube segments lea, 12h joined by welds 
44 which do not result in any surface irregularities in the 
internal or the external tube surface. Within the tube 10, 
which may be connected to a pressure medium source or sink 
at either or both ends, a piston 16 isgarrany; - A driven 
assembly 18 Meee Mere the ott side of the tube 10. 

A carriage for the goods to be conveyed, which is not 


illustrated here, can be attached to the driven assembly 18. 


ee ee es 


fhe piston 16 comprises a piston body made of aluminium 
or another light-weight non-magnetic material. This piston 
carries permanent anwar BO hich are arranged in axial 
sequence, and which are radially polarised and separated 
by spacers cc. At each end of the annular magnet arrangement 
which forms a hollow cylindrical assembly,- the piston body 
is provided with sliding guide rings 24 partially seated in 
grooves on said piston body and projecting beyond the piston 
body surface, which sliding guide rings pass along the 
internal tube surface in a sliding fit. Outside, if viewed 
axially, of these sliding juuide rings 24 are arranged elasto- 
meric sealing, rings 26, also seated in grooves on the piston 
body. bach end face oi the piston carries a buffer 28, by 
which the piston 16 cen be brought to a halt ogainst a fixed 
stop, not illustrated here. These buliers 2& further serve in 
mechanicslly linking several pistons 16, as will be described 


in more detail below. 


The external surface aress of the annular magnets 20 
are very close to the internal surtace of the tube 10. 
Guce, however, the annular magnet arrangement <O is enclosed 
on each side by a sliding guide ring 24 and a sealing ring 
26, any impurities left in the tube 10 from its manufacturing 
process or carried along with the pressure medium, (preferably 
oil or a mixture of 95 w water and 5 yw oil), cannot penetrate 
into the small jrap between the outside of the annular magnet 
arrangement of the piston and the inside of the tube. Such 


impurities might otherwise result in the piston 16 seizing 


in the Lube 10. 


Ye 
instead, any such impurities are pushed along in 
front of the piston 16 by the sealing, rings v6 or ,- should 
they manajyse to get past these * by the sliding untite rings 24, 
so that the annular magnet arrangement is invariably taced by a 


clean section oS the internal tube wall.

The end jaces 5t have a central opening 40 encircling

friction material such as » 18S provided 
an inwardly opening, groove 42 in which » wiper ring 44 is 
locuted. These wiper rings 44 ensure, in the case of the 
driven assembly 18, that the internal surface area of the 
hollow cylindrical magnet urrangement 52 is very close to 
the external surface of the tube 10, and that no foreign 
bodies can penetrate into the narrow gap between the annular 


magnels s. and the tube surface. Any such foreirn bodies, 


which commonly collect on tube surfaces not pro. ected from

Since the overall "air" gap between the internal magnet 
arrangement represented by the annular magnets 20 and the 
external magsnet arrangement represented by the annular 
maysnets 52 (this being made up of the distance between the 
anuular magnets ZO and the internal tube suriace, the wall 
thickness of the tube 10 and the distance betweer the annular 
mapnets 42 and the external tube surface) can now be kept 
very small (there is no need tor small foreign bodies to 
pass through between the magnet arrangements and the tube 
surfaces), « very good marnetic coupling can be achieved 
between the two annular magnet arranpjements, resulting 
in effective transmission of the power provided by the 


pressure medium to the driven assembly. 


As the illustration shows, there is a flush radial 
arrangement of the wiper rings 44 and the sealing rings 26, 
so that the contact force does not exert any torsional 
moment on the tube. his enables easy movement of the piston 


and the driven assembly along curved tube sections. 


megnetitaity Coupled arrangement 
The ttnear—noter, described above is easily adapted for 


transporting; heavier loads. Two pistons 16 are axially arranged 
in a tube section with auxiliary venting, the pistons butting 
against each other by their buifers v8, and driven assembly is 
provided for euch piston to be automatically wligned with 

the corresponding, piston by magnetic force. The carriage 

for the poods is then pivotally mounted on each of the 

dpiven assemblies. In this way, the two pistons now eifectively 


form a piston of double ( triple etc.) length with “articulated” 


I-21 


~~ 7 


individual pistons (held in contact by pax ceure), er suring 
synchronous movement of the two (three etc.) driven assemblies. 
ln this way, the weight of the load to be conveyed is distri- 
buted over a long tube section without, however, creating 

any passage problems such as would Occur when using corre- 
spondingly long pistons and driven assemblies in conjunction 
with the small air gaps between map:net) arrangements and 

tube surfaces which are specilied in the invention. Even 
passage along curved tube sections is possible, which would 
not be the case with one-piece pistons and driven assemblies 


of corresponding, length. 


it is obvious that this effective lengthening of the 
drivin, and the load carrying assemblies can be achieved, 
wilhout in, any way mo dy tying Lhe etongpat components of the

sialeaeeen i.e. piston 16, driven assembly 18, tor example 


on ste ri, Bi completion oJ the initial installation. 
The distribution of the loud over a longer section of the 
tube 10 is o! special importance in Lhe case of thin tubes, 
which in turn are preterable trom the point of view of the 
mayrsnetic coupling; between piston and driven assembly. Thin 
plastic tubes, otherwise being an excellent solution owing 
to their surface properties, tend to have inferior load 
carrying; capacity, which is why articulated piston and 
driven assembly arrangements are especially suitabl for 


tubes of this kind.

REQUEST FOR FIRST EXTENSION OF TIME 


Examiner D. Duggan 


Sir: 

It is respectfully requested that the shortened period 
for a response, now set to expire on October 2, 1981, be 
extended to November 2, 1981. 

In support of this request, it is pointed out that the 
undersigned receives instructions from a German patent agent 
concerning the filing and prosecution of this application. 
The instructions were received on Monday, September 28, 
which instructions were in the German language. There 
remains insufficient time for a translation to be made 
available to the undersigned attorney of record and for him 
in turn to study the translation and prepare a response for 
filing before the due date of October 2, 1981. 

In view of these circumstances, it is believed that 
additional time for filing a response is in order. Accord- 
ingly, granting of this request is respectfully solicited. 


Respectfully submitted,

In an arrangement having a hollow cylindrical tube 
and driving and driven members movable thereon for con- 
veying articles, the improvement comprising wherein said 
tube is made of a nonmagnetic material, wherein said 
driving member is a piston movably mounted on the inside 
of said tube, said piston having a piston body and plural 
axially spaced, first permanent annular magnets encircling 
said piston body, said piston further including first 
means spacing said first permanent magnets in said axial 
spaced relation, the radially peripheral surface of said 
magnets being oriented close to the internal wall surface 
of said tube, said piston further including plural guide 
ring means encircling said piston body and slidingly 
engaging said internal wall and first sealing rings located 
axially outside said guide rings for wiping said internal 
wall as said piston moves along said tube to thereby cause 
any impurities that may be present in said tube to be 
puShed along said tube so that said first annular magnets 
will be free of interference from said impurities, wherein 
said driven member includes a cylindrical sleeve made of 
a magnetizable material and encircles said tube, said 
sleeve having plural axially spaced second permanent 
annular magnets affixed thereto and in magnetically attract- 
ing relation to said first permanent annular magnets and 
second means spacing said second permanent annular magnets 
in said axially spaced relation, the radially inner surface 
of said magnets being oriented close to the external surface 
of said tube, said sleeve having end face means with second 
sealing rings located axially ».utside said second permanent 
annular magnets for wiping the external wall surface of 
said tube as said driven member is moved along said tube 
in response to a driving movement of said piston to thereby 
cause any impurities that may be present on said tube to 


be pushed along said tube so that said second permanent 


Serial No. 153 999 - page 3 


annular magnets will be free of interference from said 
impurities.

which depend therefrom, namely, Claims 2, 3, 5, 7, 9 and ll 


Accordingly, Claim 13 and the claims 


remain in this application and Applicant respectfully requests 
consideration thereof.

been reviewed for compliance with the provisions of Title 35 
usc 112. 
particularly with respect to the provisions of Title 35 USC 


Accordingly, further consideration of these claims, 


112, is respectfully solicited.

these references is believed unnecessary.

kopplung der beiden bewegbaren Teilse bewirken.

AMENDMENT UNDER RULE 312 ° 
Sir: 

Pursuant to the provisions of Rule 312, Applicant 
respectfully requests entry of the following amendment: 

Please change the title from "“MAGNETICALLY COUPLED 
ARRANGEMENT FOR A DRIVING AND A DRIVEN AND A" to 
---MAGNETICALLY COUPLED ARRANGEMENT FOR A DRIVING AND A - 
DRIVEN MEMBER---. 

REMARKS 

A routine review of this application prior to payment 
of the Issue Fee has revealed that the official Notice of 
Allowance as well as the Base Issue Fee Transmittal form for 
this application has an incorrect title printed thereon. The 
title was amended in an amendment dated November 2, 1981 and 
filed on or about November 4, 1981. 

Applicant requests that a corrected Notice of Allowance 
and Base Issu:: Fee Transmittal form be issued in this 
particular matter. If the correction has not yet been made 
and notification issued prior to the current due date for 
payment of the Issue Fee, Applicant will utilize the Base 
Issue Fee Transmittal form and modify thereon in red ink the 
title so that it is in accord with the above set forth amend- 
ment. 

Respectfully submitted, 


By ‘

Drucknittelbetitiger Linearmotor 


Die Erfindung betrif{ft einen druckmittelbetitigten Linearmotor 
fiir eine Transportvorrichtung gem4B8 dem Oberbegrift des Anspruchs 1. 


Derartige Linearmotoren werden insbesondere fiir Transport- 
vorrichtungen verwendet, und ihr Abtriebsteil ist dann mit 
einem Transportwagen fiir die zu férdernden Giiter verbunden. 
Auf diese Weise kénnen auch groBe Abmessungen aufweisende 
Giiter rohrpostaéhnlich gefiérdert werden. Line entsprechende 
Transportvorrichtung ist in der DE-OS 27 37 924 beschrieben.

Bei dem bekannten Linearmotor ist zur Sicherstellung eines 
stérungsfreien Betriebes ein gréBeres raiales Spiel zwischen 
den einander gegentiberliegenden Oberfliichen der Magnetanordnungen 
und des Rohres notwendig. Bisher war man der Auffassung, daf 
man nur auf diese Weise die stets vorhandenen Oberfliichen- 
rauhigkeiten und Querschnittsungenauigkeiten des Rohres auf- 
nehmen kann. Andererseits ware es netiirlich im Hinblick auf 
die magnetische Mitnehmkraft zwischen Kolben und Abtriebs- 
teil vorteilhaft, wenn man die GréBe des Gesamtluftspaltes 
zwischen den beiden Magnetanordnungen klein halten kéunte. 
Man kiénnte dann zur Erzielung einer gewinschten Hitneln- 
kratt zwischen Kolben und Abtriebsteil des Linearmotors 
kleinere und damit weniger schwere und insbesondere erheblich 
billigere Magnetanordnungen verwenden als bisher. 


Durch die vorliegende Erfindung soll daher ein druckmittol- 
betiitigter Linearmotor gemé& dem Oberbegriff des Anspruchs 1 
s0 weiter gebildet werden, daB eine groBe Mitnehmkraft 
zwischen Kolben und Abtriebsteil erzielt wird und trotzdem 
kein Hingenbleiben von Kolben und Abtriebsteil am Rohr 2u 


befiirchten ist. 


Diese Aufgabe ist erfindungsgem48 gelést durch einen 
Linearmotor gem&éB Anspruch ‘1. 


Es wurde erkannt, daB die heutzutage zur Verfiigung stehenden 
nicht magnetischen Rohrmaterialien eine so gute Oberflachen- 
qualitét aufweisen, daS an und fir sich der Luftspalt 


0-75 -~3- 


~-3- 
zwischen den Magnetanordnungen und den Rohroberflachen 
gegeniiber dem Stand der Technik erheblich vermindert werden 
xénnte, obwohl sich hieraus in der Praxis bisher immer nicht 
hinnehmbare Betriebsstérungen ergeben haben. Es wurde nun 
aber erkannt, daB diese Betriebsstérung nicht auf Oberfliaichen- 
rauhigkeiten und Querschnittsungenauigkeiten des Rohrmateriales 
zuriickzufiihren sind (sogar Stumpf verschweiSungen von Rohr- 
abschnitten kénnen heute mit sehr guter Oberflichengiite durch- 
gefihrt werden), sondern darauf, daB sich auf den Rohrober- 
flachen stets Schmutz und Verunreinigungen befinden, welche 
bei klein gewaihlten Spalten zwischen den Itlagnetanordnungen 
und den Rohroberfliichen nicht mehr durch diese hindurchlaufen 
kénnen sondern in diesen verkeilt werden. Dies ist bei dem 
erfindungsgemiBen Linearmotor nicht mehr m6glich, da die 
Abstreifringe des Abtriebeteliles baw. die Dichtringe des 
Kolbens den unter die Magnetanordnungen einlaufenden Teil 
der Rohroberflichen sauber halten. 


Vorteilhafte Wceiterbildungen der Erfindung sind in Unteran- 


spriichen angegeben. 


Mit der Weiterbildung der Erfindung gema’8 Anspruch 3 wird 
erreicht, da& durch den AnpreBdruck der inneren und auBeren 
Abstreifringe kein Verformen des Drehmoments. auf das kKohr 
ausgeiibt wird, welches vorzugsweise geringe Wandstarke 


haben soll, damit der Gesamtluftspalt zwischen den Hagnet- 


anordnungen klein gehalten werden kann. 


~ & 


Die Weiterbildung der Erfindung gemiB Anspruch 4 ist deshalb 
vorteilhaft, weil der Dichtring des Kolbens dann verhiltnis- 
méBig weich gewihlt werden kann und zum groBen Teil von 
Fiihrungsaufgaben befreit werden kann. Hierdurch kénnen die 
Reibungsverluste im Motor klein gehalten werden. 

Bei einem Linearmotor gemi8& Anspruch 5 erhilt man eine 
sofort mit der Druckbeaufschlagung einsetzende und sofort 
mit der Beendigung der Druckbeaufschlagung zum Stillstand 
kommende Bewegung von Kolben und Abtriebsteil, was ein sehr 
genaues Positioniocren des Abtriebsteils durch Schalten der 
Druckmittelversorgung erméglicht. AuBerdem braucht das Druck- 
mittel keinen groBen Druck aufzuweisen, da das Sowegen des 


Kolbens sher durch Mitnahme in einen Fliissigkeitestrom als 
durch Differenzdruckbeaufschlagung erfolgt. 


Gemi8B Anspruch 6 1a8t sich ein Linearmotor groBer Linge 


realisieren. 


Die Weiterbildung der Erfindung nach Anspruch 8 ist im 
Hinblick auf das Kleinhalten magnetischer Streufelder 

in der Umgebung des Abtriebsteiles von Vorteil. Auf diese 
Weise kiénnen unerwiinschte Bremskrafte verhindert werden, 
welche sonst beim Vorbeilaufen von Kolben und Abtriebs- 


teil an magnetisierbaren Maschinenteilen entstehen.

Nachstehend wird die Erfindung anhand eines Ausfiihrungs-

sowohl auf der Rohrinnenseite als auch auf der RohrauBen- 
seite keine Oberflichennnstetigkeiten bilden. Im Rohr 10, 
das wahlweise von seinen beiden Enden her mit einer Druck- 
mittelquelle bzw. einer Druckmittelsenke verbindbar ist,

Abtriebsteil des Linearmotors. Am letzteren ist eine nicht 
gezeigte Trageinrichtung fiir zu transportierende Giter be- 
festigbar.

nicht magnetischen Material mit geringem Gewicht gefertigten

Die HuBeren Mantelflichen der Ringmagnete 20 haben geringen

Das Hiilsenteil 30 umgibt axial aufeinanderfolgende Permanent-—

Die Stirnwiinde 438 haben eine das Rohr 10 unter geringem Spiel 
umgreifende mittige Offnung 40, und in deren mit einem Material 
mit niederer Reibung, z. B. Tetrafluorithylen beschichteter 
innerer Umfangswand ist eine nach innen offene Nut 42 vor- 
gesehen, in welcher ein Abstreifring 44 lauft. Die Abstreif- 
ringe 44 sorgen beim Abtriebsteil 18, bei dem die innere 
Mantelflache der hohlzylindrischen Magnetanordnung der 


I-81 


-9- ‘ 


Ringmagnete 32 nur geringen Abstand von der Oberfliache des 
Rohres 10 aufweist, dafiir, daB keine Fremdkérper in den kleinen 
Spalt zwischen den Ringmagneten 32 und der Rohroberfliche ein- 
treten kénnen. Derartige Fremdkérper, welche auf der der Un- 
gebungsatmosphiire ausgesetzten Rohroberfliche stets ange- 
troffen werden, werden also von den Abstreifringen 44 weg~- 


geschoben, bevor sie die Ringmagnete 32 erreichen. 


Dadurch, da man den gesamten "Luft-"Spalt zwischen der durch 
die Ringmagnete 20 gebildeten inneren Nagnetanordnung und der 
durch die Ringmagnete 32 gebildeten fiuferen Nagnetanordnung 
(diese besteht aus dem Abstand der Ringmagnete 20 von der 
Rohrinnenwand, der Wandstlirke des Rohres 10 und dem Abstand 
der Ringmagnete 32 von der RohrauBenwand) nun sehr klein. 
wihlen kann (kleine Fremdktrper brauchen nicht mehr zwischen 
den Magnetanordnungen und den Rohroberflachen hindurchtreten 
zu kénnen), erhélt man eine sehr gute magnetische Kopplung 
zwischen den beiden Ringmagnetanordnungen und damit eine 
gute Ubertragung der vom Druckmittel bereitgestellten Kraft 
auf das Abtriebsteil. 


Wie aus der Zeichnung ersichtlich ist, fluchten die Abstreif- 
ringe 44 und die Dichtringe 26 in radialer Richtung, so daB 
durch die Anprefkraft auf das Rohr insgesamt kein Drehmoment 
ausgeiibt wird. Durch diese Anordnung ist auch ein leichtes 
Bewegen von Kolben und Abtriebsteil tiber gebogene Rohrab- 
schnitte hinweg méglich. 


11-82 “We 


- 10 - 


Der oben beschriebene Linearmotor kann leicht fiir das 
BefSrdern schwerer Lasten modifiziert werden. Man braucht 
hierzu nur in einem Rohrabschnitt mit Hilfsentliiftung zwei 
Kolbon 16 hintereinander so anzuordnen, deB sie mit ihren 
Puffern 28 aneinander anstoBen, und fiir jeden Kolben ein 
Abtriebsteil auf das Rohr aufzuschieben, welches dann durch 
Magnetkriéfte automatisch auf den zugehérigen Kolben aus- 
gerichtet wird. Die Trageinrichtung fiir die Giiter wird dann 
gelenkig mit jedem der Abtriebsteile verburden. Die beiden 
Kolben bilden so effektiv einen Kolben doppelter (dreifacher 
uss) Linge mit gelenkig "verbundenn"(durch Druck in Anlage 
aneinander gehaltenen) Einzelkolben, und die beiden (drei usw. ) 
Abtriebsteile werden genau synchron bewegt. Auf diese Weise 
kann man das Gewicht der zu fiérdernden Last auf eine groBe 
Strecke des Rohres verteilen und erhilt trotzdem keine 
Passungsprobleme, wie sie bei der Verwendwy; entsprechend 
langer Kolben und Abtriebsteile bei den eriindungsgewid 
verwendeten kleinen luftspalten zwischen den Magnetanord- 
nungen und den Rohreberflachen erhalten wiirden. Auch gebogene 
Rohrabschnitte kénnen durchfahren werden, was wiederum bei 
Verwendung entsprechend langer einstiickiger Kolben und Ab- 
triebsteile nicht méglich ware. 


Man erkennt, daB diese durch effektive Verlingerung des 
treibenden Teiles und des die Last tragenden Teiles Linear- 
motors ohne irgendwelche Verdnderung an den Standerdteilen 
(Kolben 16, Abtriebsteil 18) miglich ist, also auch am Ein- 
satzort und nach der Erstinstallation noch ohne weiteres 

- M1 - 


o M « 


méglich ist. Die Verteilung der Last auf eine griéBere Linge 
des Rohres 10 ist von besonderer Bedeutung bei der Verwendung 
von diinnen Rohren, welche im Hinblick auf die magnetische 
Kopplung zwischen Kolben und Abtriebsteil besonders vorteil- 
haft sind. Auch diinne Kunststoffrohre, welche im Hinblick auf 
die Oberflichenbeschaffenheit an sich sehr vorteilhaft sind, 
haben in der Regel keine sehr groBe Tragkraft, und fiir sie 


eignen sich somit derartige, gelenkig verbundene Kolben und 
Abtriebsteile aufweisende Anordnungen besonders gut. 


Zum Anhalten des Abtriebsteiles bei einer gewiinschten Stelle, 
wird dort ein auf die Druckmittelversorgung zum Kohr 10 


arbeitender Stellungsgeber installiert, welcher entweder

ein auf magnetische Streufelder ansprechender Geber, z. B. 
ein Reed-Schalter sein kann.

Transport Device of a Pneumatic Dispatch Type 


The invention relates to a transport device of a pneumatic dispatch type with a tube, in which a piston- 
like part can be moved back and forth between an initial position and specific intermediate and/or end 
positions, by means of a preferably pneumatic pressure medium, where the load to be transported is 
moved along with the part. 


In the known so-called pneumatic dispatch devices, sleeves which can be closed are provided, into 
which the object to be transported is inserted, and which are then transported to a different end 


f-113 


1-112 | ) ————tt—“‘“‘Cs—sSY 


position by means of a pneumatic pressure medium. These devices have the disadvantage that the 
objects to be transported are very limited in terms of volume. Furthermore, opening and closing of 
the transport aid is relatively time-consuming and complicated. Such pneumatic dispatch devices are 
therefore only used for transport of papers, etc., over relatively long distances within a building. For 
short distances and for transport of heavier and larger loads, for example for in-house transport from 
one job site to another, devices are known which are driven, for example, by means of a pneumatic 
cylinder or by means of a chain, via a drive motor. However, pneumatic cylinders have the 
disadvantage that they can be efficiently used only to a lifting height of about 2 meters. Chain drives 


and similar devices have the disadvantage that they are very noisy in operation. 


ft is a task of the present invention to create a transport device of a pneumatic dispatch type, of the 
type indicated initially, with which relatively large objects can be transported back and forth without 


any problems, for example for purposes of in-house work sequences. 


To accomplish this task, it is provided, according to the invention, that a carriage is guided to move 
back and forth on the outside circumference of the tube made of non-magnetic material, and that the 
piston-like part and the carriage have an inside and an outside magnet, respectively, which magnets 


cause a movement coupling of the two movable parts. 


II-114 


*“-*- 3. 


The device according to the invention can therefore possess a similarly uncomplicated structure as that 
of a pneumatic dispatch device, but is practically unlimited with regard to the objects and loads to be 
transported. The device according to the invention can be used, for example, for in-house transport 
from one job site to another, for example in the form of a slanted elevator or in another manner. 
Furthermore, it produces little noise and presents itself as a closed system, in contrast to the known 
Pneumatic dispatch system, in terms of the pneumatic drive medium, so that it can have a very simple 
structure and there are no sealing probiems. 


Further details and forms of the invention are contained in the following description, in which the 
invention is described and explained in greater detail on the basis of the exemplary embodiment shown 


‘in the drawing. This shows: 


Fig. | a crosswise cross-section through the transport device according to an exemplary embodiment 
of the present invention, and 


Fig. 2 a lengthwise cross-section through the transport device according to Fig. |. 


[Translators Nete: The above description of the figures does not match the figures themselves; Fig. | 


shows a lengthwise cross-section. Fig. 2 a crosswise cross-section. } 


The transport device || of @ pneumatic dispatch type according to the invention possesses a tube |2 

made of a non-magnetic material, within which a piston-like part 13 can be moved back and forth in 
the direction of the arrow A, under the effect of a hydraulic or pneumatic pressure medium, preferably 
compressed air. The guide tube 12, which can possess a relatively small inside diameter of about 25 

mm, can have almost any desired type of path. For example, it can be guided and structured along a 
straight stretch, along a loop, level, inclined, or in any other manner. In the case of longer distances, 
it is practical to support the guide tube 12 at a constant distance with a support |4, as is evident from 


Fig. 2. 


The piston-like part 13 possesses a core 16 made of non-magnetic material, around which a magnet 
ring |7 is arranged. On both face ends of the magnet ring 17, the core 16 is surrounded by ring seals 
18 which have a radial effect, and which rest against the inside wall of the tube 12, forming a seal, 
and at the same time serve to guide the part 13. In this connection, the arrangement of the individual 
elements of the part 13 is determined in such a way that the magnet ring |7 possesses a slight ring gap 
relative to the inside wall of the tube 12, in other words is held inside the guide tube |2 in contact- 


free manner. The core 16 forms the engagement surface for the preferably pneumatic pressure 


medium on its two faces 19. 


Il-116 


On the outside circumference side, the guide tube |2 is surrounded by a carriage 21, which has the 
shape of a slit ring and is also made from non-magnetic material. The carriage 2! possesses a front 
and rear roller 22, 23, which act as transport and guide rollers, with which the carriage 2! can easily 
be moved along the length of the tube 12. The rollers 22, 23 are directed radially, with their axes of 
rotation 24 being arranged crosswise to the direction of movement. In the circumference region of the 
supports 14 which engage with the tube 12, the carriage 2! is provided with a slit 26, which 
preferably runs at a slant downward, preferably at an acute angle to the perpendicular. On the inside 
circumference side, an outside magnet ring 27 is held on the carriage 21, which ring is arranged 
coaxial to the inside magnet ring |7 and represents a counter-magnet to the latter. The outside magnet 
ring 27 is also held in such a way that its inside circumference forms a gap with the outside 
circumference of the tube 12, so that it is held in contact-free manner with regard to the tube 12. In 
the exemplary embodiment shown, the load 28 is held, i.e. attached by means of a suitable holder 
device 29, hanging down from the carriage 21. It is understood that a corresponding holder device 
can also be provided on the top, the side, or any other part of the circumference of the carriage 21. It 


is also possible to affix one or two holder devices on the face(s) of the carriage. 


0-117 


The two magnet rings 17 and 27 ose emuned to one ancther in sush 0 way that prastienily rigid 
coupling of the carriage 21 with the pneumatically driven part 23 [sic] is obtained, so that the carriage 
21 is moved back and forth synchronous with the drive of the inside part 13, for example by means of 
compressed air. The inside and/or outside magnets |7, 27 can be structured as permanent magnets or 
also as electromagnets with an adjustable force. It is also possible to combine permanent magnets and 
electromagnets with one another in such a way that the basic load is taken over by the permanent 
magnet, while electromagnets can be added or removed as desired for other cases. 


It is understood that it is also possible to give the magnets |7 and 27 a different shape. For example, 
it is possible to provide several magnets arranged to be distributed over the circumference, instead of a 
ring. It is furthermore also possible to give the carriage 21 a different shape, for example to suspend 


it from the tube in the manner of a gallows. 
It is furthermore understood that the guide tube | 2 is structured as a closed system, which is provided 
with inlet and outlet openings for the pressuve mediunr at both ends. It is also possible, depending on 


the length of the guide tube, to provide inlets and outlets for the pressure medium, which can be 


opened and shut, at intermediate stations.

APPLICATION BRANCH 90091477 


Patent: U.S. Patent No. 3,779,401 Docket No.: 163-5 
Date of Issue: December 18, 1973 Dated: March 18, 1988

STATEMENT ACCOMPANYING REQUEST FOR REEXAMINATION 
Sir: 

Reexamination under 35 U.S.C. 302-307 and 37 C.F.R. 1.510 
is respectfully requested by the patent owner of United States 
Patent No. 3,779,401, which issued on December 18, 1973 to 
George Carroll. This patent is still enforceable. 

CLAIMS FOR WHICH REEXAMINATION IS REQUESTED 

Reexamination is requested of Claims 1 through 8 of the 
Carroll patent in view of German Gebrauchsmuster No. 19 82 379. 
The German Gebrauchsmuster is also owned by the same patent 
owner. The patent owner acquired, by assignment, the Carroll 
patent for which reexamination is requested after the Carroll 
patent issued. The German Gebrauchsmuster is listed on 
attached form PTO-1449, and a copy of the German patent, as 


well as an English translation of the same, are enclosed. 


Mhver, «© the 
by “EXPRESS MAL You Otte to Aggreceee” sermes 
h | ; 
Name (Pront © Mapente fared 
II-140 


EXPLANATION OF PERTINENCY AND MANNER OF APPLYING 
CITED PRIOR ART TO CLAIMS 1 THROUGH 8, FOR WHICH 
REEXAMINATION IS REQUESTED 


It is believed that the Patent and Trademark Office may 
consider the German Gebrauchsmuster to raise substantial new 
questions regarding the patentability of Claim 1 of the Carroll 
patent, when combined with the other references of record, such 
as U.S. Patent No. 3,390,580 (Taylor). 

Claim 1 of the Carroll patent, the only independent 
claim, is repeated below: 


; 1. A device for moving articles comprising a 
cylinder of non-ferrous material, a piston 
including a permanent magnet having a pole-piece on 
each axial side thereof, a body disposed outside 
and adjacent to said cylinder, said body including 
a permanent magnet which substantially surrounds 
the cylinder, there being a pole piece on each 
axial side of the permanent magnet included in said 
body, and means for controlling the admission of 
pressure fluid into the cylinder and exhaust of 
fluid from the cylinder for moving the piston in 
the cylinder, the attractive forces between the 
permanent magnets being such that movement of the 
piston causes corresponding movement of the body 
below a predetermined load on the body and such 
that above said predetermined load movement of the 
piston does not cause corresponding movement of 
the body. 


The German patent discloses a device for transporting 
goods. The device includes a guide cylinder made from a non- 


| ae material, a piston which supports an inner magnet and 


which reciprocatingly slides within the cylinder, and an outer 
magnet mounted on the outside of the cylinder. 

The guide cylinder includes inlets and outlets for 
receiving and exhausting air or other pressure medium to effect 
reciprocal movement of the piston within the cylinder. The 
magnetic attraction between the inner and outer magnets causes 


the outer magnet to follow the piston in its movement along the 


cylinder. 


| 


I]-141 


«toe 


STATEMENT POINTING OUT SUBSTANTIAL 
NEW QUESTION OF PATENTABILITY 


The German patent referred to above is not of record in 
the prosecution file of the Carroll patent. The Carroll patent 
was assigned to the present patent owner (which also owns the 
German patent) after the Carroll patent had issued. It is 
believed that the Patent and Trademark eestes une find the 
German patent, in combination with the other references which 


were cited during the prosecution of the Carroll patent (a copy 


of each reference is also enclosed and noted on PTO form 1449), 
to disclose several of the primary structural features of the 
device defined by Claim 1. Accordingly, a substantial new 
question of patentability with respect to Claim 1 may be 
raised by the additional disclosure of the German patent. 

Claims 2 through 8 of the Carroll patent depend directly 
or indirectly from Claim 1. Because of the materiality of the 
German patent to the examination of main Claim 1 of the Carroll 
patent, and because of the dependency of the remaining claims 
of the Carroll patent on Claim 1, it is urged that a 


substantial new question of patentability may also exist with 


respect to depending Claims 2 through 8, and it is respectfully 
requested that these claims be reexamined along with Claim 1 of 


the Carroll patent. 
Respectfully submitted, 


Reg. No. 30,449 
Attorney for Patent Owner 


Hoffmann & Baron

on the piston and/or around the cylinder. 
however, preferred to arrange that the magnctic 
between the magnets should not be greatly in ex- 
of that required tu move the articles required to be 
transported. Thus should magnet 28 be obstructed by 
"a person or by some object the piston can still move to 
the end of the cylinder without any damage to the de- 
vice and without injury to the person of object. 
‘ "The device according tw the invention is capable of 
high speed operation, the speed of travel of the piston 
in the cylinder being wu function of the volume of air ad- 
mitted through the inlet port and exhausted through 
the exhaust port, this itself being a function of the size 
of the ports. By appropriate dimensioning of the ports 
it has been possible without difficulty to move a piston 
: from one end of a 20 foot cylinder to the other end 
thercof in shout 7 seconds. 

The device may be used not only for the transfer of 
articles of clothing, but may be used to carry any article 
from onc site to another along a predetermined path. 
Thus while the specific embodiment describes an ar- 
rangement suitable for transferring an article along a 
linear path, it is possible to shape the guide rod for ex- 
ample in the form of a helix s0 that the body will follow 
a helical path. 

In the embodiment described with reference to the 
drawings a polytetra Nuornethylence coating ts applied 
to the outer surface of the cylinder to provide a low 
friction surface wpon which the body may run. As an 
alternative it is possible to provide an air bearing be- 
tween the body and the cylinder. Further it is possible 
to use the repulsive force of magnets locatcd in longitu- 
dinal growves in the surface of the cylinder and further 
magnets disposed in the body which maintain the body 

- apart from said cylinder. 

If the device is designed for operation at high specds, 
it may be found that the piston is decelerated too 
abruptly on reaching the end of the cylinder. Better 
control over deceleration of the piston may be accom- 
plished by coupling the port which is exhausting to a 
chamber or cylinder containing a non-compressible 
Nuid such us water. 

The operation of the device may be triggered by any 
suitable means such as a timing device and/or a device

II-156 


The invention relates to a feeding device for transporting of goods to 
be transported between an initial position and given end or intermediary 
position being driven with the aid of a pressure medium as the movement 
generator,for example,a pneumatic or hydraulic medium. 


Devices of the type in question are used,for example,as as hoisting like 
stroke devices,suspended four post like transport devices etc. 


The known devices of the aforementioned type have a complicated 
structure in combination with Large manufacturing costs and require a 
relatively large space requirement at the place of installation. Among 
others,and for this reason the use of these known devices is rather Limited. 
It is therefore an object of the invention to overcome these disadvantages. 


With respect to the aforementioned purpose the new feeding device in 
accordance with the invention is provided with a reciprocally movable | 
piston without a piston rod in a tube or a pressure or operating cylinder 
between the initial position and the end position having the effect of 
@ pressure medium and a reciprocally movable holding device for the goods 
to be transported being mounted outside cf the tube or the pressure or 
operating cylinder being in a magnetic force connection and oerating 
connection with the piston. The new device may contain,for example,a reci- 
procally movable piston which moves under the effect of the pressure 
medium,a guide tube containing the piston acting as a pressure or operating 
cylinder made from a nonmagnetic material with inlets and outlets for 
the pressure medium,an inner magnet which is connected with the piston and 
guided along the inner wall,an outer magnet on the outer wall of the guide 


0-157 


' 
re 
' 


"being reciprocally movable at the area of the inner magnet and may 
' also contain a reciprocally movable holding device for the goods to be 
‘transported at the outer magnet. The device in accordance with the in= 
vention,which is characterized by a very simple structure,enable to cut 
the space requirement to practically half of the space requirement with 
respect to known devices. For example,in hoisting like devices,stroke 


operated tables etc.,there is only a structural length required which 


corresponds to the stroke length ,while in the known comparable devices the 


installation length is about double of the length of the stroke length,so 
that customarily in such cases one buries the drive cylinder into the 
ground. The application range of pneumatic or hydraulic operating cylinders 
in cases of the type in question are substantially expanded with the inven 
tive device,since one can now operate with overlong piston rods,for 
example,of 10 meters and more,without being afraid to cause instability, 
while with the known devices a piston rod length of maximal 2 - 3 metters 
The holding device of the inventive device is also movable 
around curves,whereby the guiding is simple,while with suspended feéding — 
devices a lateral friction is caused in the case of curves by the rigid 


are possible. 


element.

Moreover, ‘the device in accordance with the ionetniin also contains an

as an outer magnet 5 which is fed contact free from the outside along the. 
outer wall of the tube and between it and the piston is movable along the 
guide tube thanks to the magnetic force connection.A reciprocally movable 
holding device 6 for the goods to be transported is provided with the 
outer magnet being fed with a rolling friction on the guiding tube, for 
example. 


In the embodiment in accordance with Fig.l to 3 the piston 1 consists 
of a core la, which may be made,for example,from a nonmagnetic material 
like synthetic material and of packing rings lb,lc which engage with their 
outer face or cirfumferential face on the inner tube wall and which act for 
a tight sealing of space 7 from space 8 within the guiding tube 3. The 
piston supports an inner magnet 4 which in this case is shaped as a magnetic 
ring. However,the inner magnet may also be shaped as a magnetic as a 
molded body,as is illustrated at 9 in Fig.4a,for example. In this latter 
case the piston is mounted on the inner wall of the guide tube with the 
assistance of a plurality of guide rollers,for example,three guide rollers 
10a,10b,10c,whose rotational axis lla,llb,lle extend transversely to the 
longitudinal axis and at an angle with respect to each other and which

In the device in accordance with Figs.1 to 3,the outer magnet 5 is 
shaped as a magnetic molded body,whose active face 5a facing the guide 
tube 3 is correspondingly shaped with the associated segment of the.‘tube

ring extending in a coaxial arrangement. It covid also be shaped as a not

outer magnet may also be shaped as a completely closed ring 16 which comple- 
tely encompasses the guide tube 17. While in the device in accordance with 
Fig.5 a space is provided for jolding devices of the guide tube within 
the movement path of the magnet,see for example,the holding devices 16 in

is thought for cases wherein the guide tube,which extends only over a short 
axial length,is held at its both ends,whereby the transport movement

fere with the movement of the outer magnet 16. In the devices in accordance

INATION NO. 3,779,401 


DECISION 


A substantial new question of patentability 
affecting claims 1-8 of United States Patent Number 
3,779,401 is raised by the request for reexamination. 

Extensions of time under 37 CFR 1.136(a) will not be 
permitted in these proceedings because the provisions of 
37 CFR 1.136 apply only to “an applicant” and not 
parties in a reexamination proceeding. Additionally, 
Office policy requires that reexamination proceedings 
“will be conducted with special dispatch" (37 CFR 
1.550(a)) and provides for extensions of time in 
reexamination proceedings as set forth in 37 CFR 
1.550(c). 

German Gebrauchsmuster No. 1,982,379 (newly cited 
in the Request for Reexamination) discloses an article 
feeding device comprising a body including an article 
transport device (or holder) and a magnet movably 
mounted on the outside of a non-magnetic cylinder 
wherein a magnetic piston inside the cylinder is moved 
back and forth in response to the supply and relief of 
hydraulic fluid within the cylinder, thereby moving the 


article transport device. The Taylor patent of record 


in the prosecution of U.S. patent no. 3,779,401 does not 


disclose an article transport device but rather a 
magnetic indicator on the outside of a cylinder which 
follows a magnetic piston within the cylinder to 
indicate the position of the magnetic piston. 

As German document 1,982,379 discloses an article 
transport device which is movable in response to a 
hydraulically operated magnetic piston, which is a 
feature that was not found by the Examiner during the 
prosecution of the application which matured 


into the subject patent, and this feature is 


II-178 


CONTROL NUMBER 001477 -3 
REEXAMINATION NUMBER 3,779,401 


material to the patentability of all of the claims in the 
patent, it is therefore determined that a substantial 


new question of patentability has been raised.

REMARKS 
The order granting reexamination and the comments of the 
Examiner in the decision attached to the order are noted and 


-2- 
-181 


iated. The claims of the Carroll patent have been amended 
in a sincere effort to define more clearly and more specifically 
fea_ures of patentee's invention that distinguish over the art of 
record, including German Gebrauchsmuster No. 1,982,379, which was 
newly cited by the patent owner in its Request for Reexamination. 

Main claim 1 of the Carroll patent has been replaced by 
new claim 9 submitted herewith for the Examtner's consideration. 
As will be discussed below, it is respectfully urged that the 
structure now set forth with particularity in claim 9 is not 
found in the art of record or in the German Gebrauchsmuster. 

New claim 9 sets forth the structure of a device which may 
be used to transport articles, such as garments or clothing, from 
one site to another, and thus is particularly useful in the 
clothing industry where light loads are required to be moved at 
high speeds (Col. 1, lines 13-16 of the Carroll patent). With 
reference to a preferred form of the device illustrated by the 
drawings of the Carroll patent, claim 9 defines the device for 
moving articles as including a hollow cylinder (for example, 
cylinder 10 illustrated by Fig. 1) having opposite axial ends, 
and a piston (for example, piston 18) which is mounted in the 
interior of the cylinder and which slides back and forth in the 
cylinder. 

Claim 9 specifically defines the piston as including a 
central mounting member (for example, bolt 21 shown in Fig. 3), 4 
plurality of cylindrically-shaped permanent magnets (for example, 
magnets 20 shown in Figs. 3 and 5) mounted on the central 
mounting member, at least one pair of end members (members 22 
shown in Fig. 3) mounted on the central mounting member and 
disposed on opposite axial sides of the plurality of magnets, and 
a pair of resilient sealing rings (rings 26 shown in Fig. 3) 
situated near opposite axial ends of the mounting member and 
engaging the cylinder to provide a fluid-tight seal. 


Zo II-182 


Claim 9 also now more specifically defines the article 
moving device as including a pair of cushion members formed of 
resilient material. Such cushion members are illustrated by Fig. 
3 of the Carroll patent and described in the patent 
specification, at Column 4, lines 13-16 thereof. Claim 9 
specifically defines the cushion members as being situated near 
opposite axial ends of the central mounting member (i.e., bolt 
21) to help prevent damage to the piston when the piston contacts 
an axial end of the cylinder. 

Claim 9 also specifically defines the article moving 
device of the Carroll patent as including a body mounted on the 
exterior of the cylinder and being reciprocatingly slidable on 
the cylinder. The body is defined as including a plurality of 
annularly shaped permanent magnets (for example, magnets 28 shown 
in Figs. 3 and 5) surrounding the cylinder. Claim 9 defines the 
permanent magnets of the piston and body as being polarized, as 
shown in Figs. 4 and 5, so as to magnetically couple the body to 
the piston. Thus, and as set forth in new claim 9, movement of 
the piston inside the cylinder causes a corresponding movement of 
the body outside the cylinder. 

Claim 9 also defines the body mounted on the cylinder as 
further including means for holding an article to be moved on the 
body. Such means can be in the form of the gripping device 30 
shown in Fig. 1 of the Carroll patent. 

Claim 9 further defines the article moving device of the 
Carroll patent as including means for controlling the admission 
of pressure fluid into the cylinder and exhaust fluid from the 
cylinder for moving the piston in the cylinder. An example of 
such fluid controlling means is the five part spool valve 40 
illustrated by Fig. 1 of the drawings. 

As in original claim 1, new claim 9 defines the attractive 
forces between the permanent magnets of the piston and the body 


-4- 1-183 


as being such that movement of the piston causes 4 corresponding 


Imovement of the body below a predetermined load on the body and 
|such that above said predetermined load, movement of the piston 


jdoes not cause corresponding movement of the body. 


It is respectfully urged that the structure now set forth 
jin new claim 9 patentably distinguishes over the references of 


| record in the Carroll patent and newly cited German 
lcebrauchssuster No. 1,982,379. 

The German patent discloses a device for transporting 

| goods. with reference to Figs. 1 and 2 of the German patent, the 
jdevice includes a guide cylinder or tube 3 made from a non- 

| magnetic material, a piston l which supports an inner magnet 4 
jJand which reciprocatingly slides within the cylinder, and an 
jouter magnet 5 mounted on the outside of the cylinder. The guide 
lcylinder 3 also includes inlets and outlets for receiving and 

| exhausting air or other pressure medium to effect reciprocal 
jmovement of the piston within the cylinder. The magnetic 
jattraction between the inner and outer magnets causes the outer 
lmagnet to follow the piston in its movement within the cylinder. 
It is respectfully urged that the particular structure of 
jthe inner piston and outer body now more specifically set forth 
= new claim 9 is not taught or suggested by the German patent. 
jIn particular, claim 9 defines the piston as including a 


|plurality of spaced apart magnets, each in a cylindrical form, 
jand also defines the outer body as including a plurality of 
spaced apart magnets, each in an annular form, as illustrated by 
| rig. 5 of the Carroll patent. None of the references cited 

| during prosecution of the Carroll patent discloses the structure 
lot an outer body formed of a plurality of spaced apart annular 
Imagnets and a piston formed of spaced apart cylindrical magnets, 
nor does the German patent disclose this structure. The German 


patent discloses a single inner magnet 4 and a single outer 


| 


magnet 5. The advantage of having not only a plurality of 
magnets on both the piston and the outer body but also spacing 
the magnets of the piston and body apart from each other is to 
increase the magnetic coupling between the two so that heavier 
loads may be transported by the outer body. 

New claim 9 now also more specifically defines the 
article transport device of the Carroll patent as including 
cushions of resilient material mounted on the axial ends of the 
piston. As disclosed at Column 2, lines 13-16 of the Carroll 
patent specification, and as illustrated by Fig. 3 of the patent, 
the cushions are provided to prevent damage to the pneumatic 
device when the piston reaches the ends of the cylinder. 

None of the references cited during prosecution of the 
Carroll patent discloses not only the overall structure of the 
article transport device set forth in claim 9, but also a pair 
of resilient cushions mounted on axial ends of a piston that is 
movable within a cylinder. Similarly, the German patent does not 
disclose resilient cushions mounted on the ends of piston 1. 
Accordingly, it would be possible for the piston of the device 
disclosed in the German patent to be damaged if it struck the 
closed ends of the cylinder. 

In summary, new claim 9, which replaces original claim 1 
of the Carroll patent, is respectfully urged to patentably 
distinguish over the references cited during prosecution of the 
Carroll patent and to distinguish over the German patent 
submitted by the patent owner for the Examiner's consideration in 
that the references and the German patent do not disclose the 
overall structure of the device for moving articles set forth in 


the claim, and in particular do not disclose 1) a plurality of 


cylindrically-shaped permanent magnets mounted on a central 


jmounting member and spaced apart axially from each other, where 
+. 
each magnet has a bore formed axially therethrough for receiving 


the central mounting member, 2) an exterior body including a 


plurality of annularly shaped permanent magnets spaced apart from 


each other and 3) a pair of cushion members formed of resilient 
material and situated near opposite axial ends of the central 
mounting member of the piston to help prevent damage to the 
piston when the piston contacts an axial end of the cylinder, as 
specifically set forth in new claim 9 submitted herewith. 

Claim 2 of the Carroll patent has been cancelled, as the 
structure set forth in the claim has now been incorporated into 
new proposed claim 9. 

Claim 3 has been amended so that it now directly depends 
from new claim 9. | 

Claim 5 has also been amended so that it now depends from 
new claim 9. 

Claims 7 and 8 have been cancelled, as the structure set 
forth in these claims has now been incorporated into new 
proposed claim 9. 

It is respectfully urged that the remaining dependent 
Claims, that is, claims 3 through 6, as amended, patentably 
distinguish over the references cited in the Carroll patent and 
over the German patent for the reasons submitted above with 
respect to new main claim 9. 

In view of the foregoing amendments and remarks, entry of 
new main claim 9 and the amendments to claims 3 and 5, and the 
issuance of a reexamination certificate with claims 3 through 6 
and 9 are respectfully solicited. 

Respectfully submitted, 


T. Brobur 


T. Bodner’ 
Attorney for Patent Owner

MANUALLY OPERATED HYDRAULIC ACTUATOR CONTROL HAVING FEEL-BACK

IIl-206 


connection with the gripping tongs or jaws of 
controlled manipulators or mechanical arms and the like. 


arms ordinarily include an upper arm member rotatably 
on a base and provided with drive motors for controlling 
the motions thereof. Pivotally mounted on the end 


. A pair of 
gripping tongs or jaws are provided at the free end of 
the lower arm. The jaws are opened and closed by a 
suitable remotely controlled actuator. The present in- 
vention provides an improved method for opening and 
closing such jaws with a provision for allowing the opera- 


operator through a system of pulleys, cables and levers. 
These devices can exert only the force the operator exerts 
less all the frictional effects which, because of the system's 
complexity, may be considerable and ‘may vary appre- 
ciably from time to time. The second is power devices, 
i.e. electrically powered or hydraulically powered devices, 
which utilize a meter or sound system for measuring pres- 
sure exerted. These devices are satisfactory, but require 
extra attention, i.e. observation or listening, by the opera- 
tor. The third type of device is the electrical servo sys- 
tem which utilizes an electromotive device such as a D.C. 
motor to resist the operator's grip. This sort of system 
has been used, among other places, in aircraft but is 
usually rather complicated and quite expensive. Further- 
more, since the output device often operates in a statioa- 
ary or stalled condition, these electrical devices often tend 
to overheat. The present invention, in contrast, can op- 
erate in a stalled condition indefinitely, yet has no ten- 
dency to overheat, and is cheaper and easier to manu- 
facture than these electrical devices. Still another type 
of device utilizes a direct mechanical linkage to the out- 
put device but is provided with a power assist mechanism 
coupled to the linkage to increase the power output. This 
type of system is, however, limited in its application since 
the output device cannot be moved from place to place 
with respect to the control device because of the direct 
mechanical linkage. The present invention is more satis- 
factory in this respect since flexible hydraulic hoses may 


10 


15 


~ 
o 


2 
be used which allow the manipulator to be moved in all 
directions with respect to the remotely placed control. 
A further disadvantage of previous devices is the in- 
ability to quickly and conveniently change the ratio be- 
tween the pressure exerted by the output actuator and 

the tactile pressure detected by the operator. 

It is therefore one object of this invention to provide an 
improved manually operated hydraulic actuator control 
which provides for the operator a tactile sense of pressure 

— exerted by the actuator. 
is another object of the present invention to provide 
an improved manually operated actuator control with 
feel-back which can exert a pressure of many times the 
strength of the human band and in which the output de- 
vice or actuator can be moved from place to piace as 
It is still another object of the present invention to

In the embodiment illustrated by FIGURE 4, the flask 
126 is preferably permanently installed adjacent a system 
to be sampled; as for example, a fuid carrying pipe line 
10. Fluid conduit 25 operatively connects the system to a 
pump meuns 12 such as seen in FIGURE 1. After the 
sample flask 126 is filled with fluid from the conduit 10, 
the solenoid coil 128 may be suitably excited and moved 
longitudinally of the flask so as to move the perforated 
plate 149 between end walls 150 and 151 by the magnetic 
force exerted by solenoid coil 128 in order to blend the 
contents of the flask. Thereafter, valves 110, 263, and 264 
are opened so as to fill laboratory sample flask 226 with 
a homogeneous fluid sample from chamber 148’. Transfer 


plied current of solenoids 28 is regulated by the adjust- 55 of liquid from chamber 148’ into chamber 248 may be 


able orifice 39, 40 located in passageway 36. The response 
rate of the armature to the current applied to solenoids 
28 and 28’ is increased as the rod 40 is withdrawn from 
the counter-bore 39 which forms part of the orifice or 


adjustable passageway. The length of the pump stroke is gp 


controlled by adjustable rod 42 which provides a cush- 
ioned stop for the armature. As rod 42 is removed from 
its bore, the stroke of the pump is increased, and con- 
versely, as the rod is extended into the pump head, the 
length of the stroke is decreased. Hence rods 41 and 42 
provide a means by which both the rate of response and 
the length of the stroke of the piston may be adjusted 
to control the pumping time during a cycle as weil as the 
integral sample size that is transferred from the pipe 
line 10 into the flask 26. 

The rate at which the armature is actuated, or the num- 
ber of strokes per minute made by the pump, is governed 
by control unit 4, which is adapted to energize the coils 
28 and 28’ with a sivnal that is sent through the electrical 
conduits 2, 2’, 3, and 3’.

fluid will flow through valve 110, quick disconnect 112,

116, valve 263, and hence into chamber 248 thereby dis-

sample flask 226, valves 264 and 263 are closed by using 
a suitable valve handle that mates with valve stem 265,

12. A method of transferring a fluid from a pipeline or 

the like into a sample flask comprising: 

(a) transferring a predetermined amount of fluid sam- 
ple at a predetermined rate from said pipeline into a 
first flask;

ous fluid sample representative of the average fluid 
conditions of said flask; 

(c) magnetically moving a piston to move a portion 
of the fluid from said first flask into a second flask to 
thereby provide a sample size suitable for laboratory 
determination;
